(10) Deferred: both handlers ignore `params`, with the `window` parameter and `origin_cell` reserved for doc 16 section 11's 10 km path. The 2 s map self-heal is a client rule for Q.4 and Q.5. It is mandatory, because a query reply is conn-scoped and never resumes, so `arena.map`'s `retain` marker is inert today. No vitest test reads the fixtures until Q.5. The retain rule does not look at `PanelFrameKind`, so a future marked `Delta` would evict its own base. The 256 KiB map bound is a test, not an enforcement. The parity scene's double clamp puts the window origin at (0,0), so Q.4 and Q.5 each owe one composition case at a non-zero origin. The mush-session crate-level resume paragraph does not yet mention superseded keyframes. (11) The gateway half changed, so dev1 takes it with a restart of `dev1mush-gateway` and not a reload. A mixed deploy is safe in both orders. **Q.4.** (1) The parity test hands the renderer a 78 by 22 pane, not the brief's (78, 20): the renderer reserves `MAP_CHROME_ROWS` = 2 for its heading and legend so it renders exactly its area, because a grid sized to the full inner height would have `draw_pane` clip the window's southernmost row, which is where a pilot on the south edge is drawn; the composed grid is still exactly 78 by 20 and equals `view_grid.txt`. (2) The map heading is the pane's first content row and not the border title, so the renderer stays a pure function of frame, terrain and pane size. (3) The tab gate needs a held seated frame as well as the manifest (new `Screen.gate` plus sticky `cockpit_seat`): the manifest is server-wide and over SSH is always empty, and the brief's own observer assertion wants a bystander to have no tab; sticky so the `seated: false` empty state has a screen to be on, at the cost of a tab that outlives the seat until `@notui`. (4) `entry_queries` asks `arena.cockpit`, which the brief did not list: the arena pushes only changed frames, so a stationary pilot would otherwise never get a screen; on a world with no tacsim this earns one `Unknown query` line over SSH, as `comsys` and `scenes` already do. (5) Contact glyphs are derived from `class`/`friendly` by `ContactRow::glyph`'s rule, not read off the fixture, because `cockpit_frame.json` carries no glyph; that is a second copy of the rule in the TUI. (6) The legend is in frame order (`x BA + RB ? X3`), not the brief's illustrative order, and it still names a marker a later one overwrote. (7) `GLOBAL_KEYS` gained `'0'` (15 to 16), unlisted but required. (8) `notices` sits in the Left column and `right_shares_surplus` is true, so the map keeps the Main column's height but only half the width surplus. (9) Scan words carry their band letter when not green (`hull/y`) so a colourless terminal loses nothing, and the degree mark degrades with the em dash without UTF-8. (10) Beyond the brief, from review: every selectable pane's highlight is keyed on `row_key`; the self-heal latch re-arms only on a match change; the `arena.map` `on_enter` ask is adopted by the heal and skipped when the ground is held, so `arena.map` is gated by the held frame, not the manifest. (11) Deferred: the observer test does not assert absence of the pilot name, team word or non-`@` marker glyph before detection (short words collide with painted text) and its after-detection needle can be met by the notice line; the `None` fallback branch is untested; no split-gateway test opens screen 0; rows clip instead of wrapping, so the scan and damage rows lose their tails in a 34-column pane and an 80-column terminal loses `weapons` and `contacts`; `v` has no grid surface on the TUI; `docs/interface/02-implementation-plan.md` still says `MAX_SCREENS` is 9. No departure from doc 16 beyond those Q.3 already named; §5's parity is met by an equality to the line renderer's grid, which is stronger than a per-renderer golden. **Q.5.** One must-fix. `web/src/lib/cockpit.ts` carried literal C0/DEL/C1 bytes, a NUL among them, inside `clean`'s character class, so git classed the cut's central module as binary. It was rewritten with `\u` escapes and proved by `git diff --no-index --numstat` (780 lines), a 22-file byte sweep finding none, and a new decoder case. Seven should-fix items were fixed: the hello pass's `arena.map` ask now arms the latch, so an attach pulls the terrain once and not twice; the auto-switch is owed and waits out a focused composer; the stale four-verbs sentence; the help page's web-only facts; a whitelist probe in place of a blocklist; the `activeDef` assertion asked of a gate the cockpit passes; and the prefill path pinned by a test. One was deferred to the owner. The gate was green on the first round. (1) A prefill leaves screen 0: `focusComposer` switches to the scene screen because the web has one composer and `SceneScreen` mounts it, where the TUI keeps the pilot on the cockpit. The brief asked for prefill only and a second input surface is new scope, so it is pinned by a test, said in help and README, and left for an owner ruling. (2) `ComposedWindow` carries one field beyond the brief, `terrain`, because `+` is the door colour and `x` is rubble, so painting ground from `grid` would lie under every marker; `grid` stays the parity subject. (3) There is no component-render test, because vitest runs in node with no jsdom and adding one is a lockfile change. It is substituted by the view-model golden, the decoy case and a whitelist source probe, the client's existing convention. (4) The web's tab gate is the manifest alone, where the TUI also wants a sticky seated frame. That is observer-safe because the empty state is byte-identical and the query answers `seated: false`, and help states the rule per client. (5) The once-per-match switch waits while a mounted composer has focus, which the brief did not ask for, because it is the client's only server-driven switch and unmounting the composer takes the draft, the sticky mode and the history. (6) `arena.map` is hydrated on the hello pass as well as self-healed, the hello's ask armed as a wildcard through `noteMapAsked`. (7) `MapCanvas.svelte` and `map.svelte.ts` were touched outside the brief's list to lift one shared `TERRAIN_COLOURS`, which also paints five classes the atlas canvas drew as nothing. (8) `fixtures/cockpit/README.md` was edited (one row, one bless line) although the brief says not to edit the fixtures. It is the index, no Rust test reads it, and the payload files are untouched. (9) Canvas designator labels are drawn only at 6 px a cell and up, so not at the default 128-cell view; the legend line names the markers there. (10) The zoom buttons' `−` and `+` follow the cell count and not the usual in/out sense; their aria-labels are truthful. (11) Left as recorded notes and not fixed: an unknown scan band letter paints green; `applyMap` lets another match's answer replace a held map until the next ask; the deploy staleness check is an mtime heuristic over `web/src` that a rollback passes, that ignores vite inputs outside it and cannot see a gateway binary built before the bundle, and the README's reason for the ship-elsewhere route is wrong though the refusal fails safe; the canvas repaints static terrain on every pulse; the marker-overwrite rule, the palette and the canvas painting are unpinned; and `deploy.sh` was only `bash -n`-checked and never run. (12) No cross-tempo test and no harness run, because no Rust source changed. Doc 16 section 5's wording holds, and its parity requirement is met by the third renderer reproducing `view_grid.txt`, not by a new golden. **Q.6.** One must-fix (no fixture ever jammed the SHARER's end of the mesh, so stubbing `sharer_jam` to `None` left the tree green; fixed by `q6_a_pod_beside_the_sharer_cuts_the_mesh_at_both_terms`, mutation-verified) and nine should-fix. Five were fixed: the two-cell rationale withdrawn from `clock.rs` and the one-cell guard and re-derived as margin; the edge test given a rolling-carry drive at 4,999 cm/s; the zeroing test moved to 30,000 mdeg so `carry.north` is really carried into each refusal; the cockpit repeat loop pumping before it reads, with `weapons.rs` (5b) carrying the ledger half; `UnitResult::hits`'s doc. Four were deferred with reasons below. Gate green first time, p99 8.194 ms. (1) The brief's headline assertion of EQUAL ledger `blocked` counts at every partition was NOT written, because its premise is false. The counts are `[1, 6, 278, 1]` over (5000,250), (5000,150), (5000,50) and (370,250), and X.6's fixture still prints 1 against 5 exactly as before the carries. The cause is cut F.2's two-flag split: `step.rs` journals on the rising edge of the per-substep `blocked` flag and tells the pilot on the rising edge of `held`. The carry that would close the gap is the one a refusal must not bank. The test asserts what is true (the same centimetre, held, the pilot told once at all four partitions) and prints the counts with only `>= 1` asserted. The remedy (gate the journal on `!was_held`, and fold `held` into `state_hash`) changes what the hash covers and is deferred to a ruling before Q.7. A stop is therefore NOT one `Blocked` ledger event at every substep length, and Q.7's blocked-cell memory must be idempotent per cell or read the `held` edge; the Q.6 brief's closing paragraph said the opposite. (2) Doc 16 §3.1's substep formula becomes `(cell_cm - 1) x 1000 / max_speed_cms`. The brief's stated reason (a carry rollover making a two-cell step under the old bound) is arithmetically false: `cell_at` divides and the carry is inside the one quotient, so the old bound was also one cell. The change is kept as a strict margin and said as such. `16-combat-arena.md:133` and the cut-A row at `16b:384` still print the old quotient and are the committer's to amend. (3) Doc 16 §3.6's amendment note (`16-combat-arena.md:647`, that the share mesh is not jammed and that one W6 clause is still open) and the cut-X row at `16b:391` (the mesh deliberately not jammed; the four cross-tempo tests still owed) are now false and are the committer's to strike. W6 is closed and X.2 to X.5 are paid. (4) Doc 16 §4's result record: `hits` is now rounds that took something off something and differs from the black box's `hit` lines by exactly the rounds spent on spent sections; the record's prose should say so. (5) The harness `blocked` counter ROSE (250 to 288) where the brief expected a fall: ten more units are under way at the end because a speed that truncated to zero centimetres a substep now accumulates, and units that move reach obstacles. This is a new baseline row and not a regression. (6) The four owed cross-tempo tests sit in one `q6_` block at the end of `extensions.rs` and not beside each item's acceptance, because three of them need a 16 by 48 corridor and the mesh needs a blind mate. (7) The owner-ruling SSH witness is not the brief's lock-inside-the-window-then-FIRE. It re-issues `LOCK` under the word the lock was first taken under and checks `WEAPONSTATUS`. That holds in both branches of a 250 ms polling race against a 3 s window, but it proves the alias only in one branch; the FIRE-with-no-re-lock half already existed in that lens and the two library tests carry the clock-driven proof. The cockpit test waits on real arena time, because `classify_after_ms` is content, and is load-sensitive. (8) `MotionCarry` derives `Serialize, Deserialize` beyond the brief and nothing uses them; the carries have no chosen wire form. (9) Doc comments the brief did not name were corrected (`mush-config`'s `substep_max_ms`, `UnitReturn.hits`, `UnitResult.hits`, X.6's test doc). Left stale: `motion.rs`'s head and `MotionCarry`'s doc still cite X.6's five-against-one as evidence of the truncation, which the cut's own measurement refutes; three comments still say the carry adds at most one (a loose but true bound); and a bare `SIN_ONE` intra-doc link in `state.rs` does not resolve. (10) Deferred notes: `share_pass` folds the sharer's jamming above the nothing-to-share return and the mate's above the sight test (free in a stock match, one extra index scan each in a pod-bearing one); there is no test of a mate exactly at the share reach, none that a stop leaves `carry.turn` alone, and none of a reversing unit (Euclidean floor moves its first centimetre one substep early, bounded at 1 cm and partition-independent). (11) Not done by design: `MIN_SUBSTEP_MS` can still beat the one-cell bound above 20,000 cm/s on 10 m cells, where motion's guard refuses the jump; the absolute substep lattice is not built, so two different partitions of one arena time still give two event lists (an owner question); and the boot pod sweep was not moved. **Q.7.** The gate was green on round 1: nine commands, no reruns, engaged p99 7.596 ms against 12. The lenses raised two must-fix and ten should-fix findings. All were fixed or rebutted, and the judge shipped with notes. (1) `deploy/dev1/mushd.toml` does not exist. dev1 has rendered from `deploy/instance/mushd.toml.tmpl` since F.5, so `ai_fill = true` and `rally_ms` went into the template and dev2, and the example stays off. (2) `Spent` has a gate the brief did not name: each side needs an in-action unit with `UnitState::armed_at_checkout()`. Read literally, the predicate is true at millisecond zero for every unarmed-target fixture and for the harness movers; five tests failed and one hung without the gate. (3) The dry `Withdraw` edge is gated the same way through `is_dry`, because an unarmed platform patrols by `best_hostile`'s own ruling. (4) The stand applies to a commanded `Objective::Withdraw` too. `a_withdraw_order_withdraws_and_holds_fire` was renamed and its fire assertion inverted, and narrowed to RED_A. `hold_fire` is the commander's way to say do not shoot, and after the fix it also suppresses the stand. Doc 16 §6 "Deliberation" owes that sentence. (5) correctness/Q7-1 was rebutted on the brief's own words and carried forward, not fixed. The stand is gated on full `order_reach_cm`, so a hurt machine with a live line to a classified hostile stands and duels, and cannot rally while it holds one. The rebuttal's claim that classification comes only from own sensors is wrong, because `share_pass` classifies too. An owner ruling after a playtest is wanted. (6) X.6 deviation 13 is NOT closed at the stock doctrine. On `plateau(true)` at `ArenaConfig::default()` and the stock helm, the run still measured seven `Blocked` cells, five on the cliff line. The cause is the 1 Hz decide cadence at 15 m/s. The brief's "X.6's fixture, stock doctrine, zero `too_steep` stops" test cannot be written green. It was replaced by a synthetic bluff at `repath_ms` 0, and the X.6 comments were rewritten to say so. (7) The stand's candidate is `best_hostile(prefer = None)` and not `row.target`, which the Withdraw edge clears. `row.target` stays unset. (8) The rally timer runs only once the unit has arrived or has no goal. (9) `SIM AI` needs lead or staff even for the bare read. `AiOff` is checked before `WrongState` and `NotLead`. A non-numeric argument answers a usage sentence the brief did not spell. (10) After the fix, `ai_side_size` reads the stored number as a FLOOR under `largest_side`, so a claim made after `SIM AI` raises both sides together. (11) `Spent` is carried on `Centre.outcome_reason` and does not widen `MatchOutcome`; doc 16 §4's six words stand. (12) After the fix, `would_overheat` is the engine's own line: `> max`, and a unit with no heat cell is never held back. The brief's wording would have been one shot stricter than the engine. (13) `first_step_is_open` reads the octant of the heading, not the `signum` of the cell delta. `shoulder_refuses` was made `pub(crate)` rather than copied. `path_with` keeps a flat argument list under an `#[allow]`. (14) `crates/mush-gateway/tests/arena_story_split.rs` moved `summary_ms` from 200 to 1000. It is the only arena test that boots through the mapper, and nothing in it reads a summary. (15) `sim_solo.rs` asserts no kill and no `match_ended` line, because the tail chunk is still in the egress ring. After the fix, its observer-rule block makes claims that hold at any arena time (`LOCK BB`, no object name, no sixteen-digit word), because the cold-platform read raced the `id % pass_ms` sensor stagger. The cold-platform claim and the frame JSON are left to `observer_rule.rs`. (16) The second cross-tempo log covers withdraw, stand, dwell and rally, and has no re-engage leg. The hostile pulls out at arena millisecond 20,000. (17) The ragged-partition test compares the avoided-cell SET and that both runs got through the causeway, not an end cell, because Q.6 showed position is not partition-exact. (18) `blocked` assertions are counted per obstacle cell. The re-search was NOT rate-limited to `repath_ms / 4`, because p99 held. `blocked` rose 288→304 against the brief's guess and `path_queries` stayed flat. (19) `sim_lobby.rs` carries only the fill-off half of the lobby claims. The fill-on claims are in `match_lifecycle.rs`, `sim_reflex.rs` and `sim_solo.rs`. (20) Known small items left open: a standing Withdraw order re-enters `Withdraw` the tick after a rally, flipping to `Patrol` for one tick every `rally_ms`; `Spent` has no cross-tempo test; `NobodyPlaying` is unreachable and the two centre-wide abort sentences are unasserted; the `avoid` clause of `first_step_is_open` is untested, and its doc overstates parity with motion's step 4; the validator table has no ladder-consistent `quiet_ms` row; the validators run with `[tacsim].enabled = false`; the help omits the arrival and dry conditions of the rally. (21) Doc 16 amendments owed by the committer: §3.8 `Selection` "two to" becomes "one to"; `Boarding` "at least one pilot per side" becomes "at least one claimed slot, and with `ai_fill` off one per side"; §3.8 `Running`'s end enumeration gains `spent`, a draw; §6's machine chain gains the dry edge, the in-state stand and the one rally edge `Withdraw → Patrol`; §6 "Deliberation" gains the cornered stand under an ordered withdraw; §11's N.1 entry says the fill is sized. §4 needs nothing. (22) Deploy: server half only, and the binary goes out before the rendered config. `rally_ms` is a new key under `deny_unknown_fields`, and the template now turns the fill on. **Q.8.** (1) The brief's `SIM STATUS` example `1 (RA): Alice [biped_mk1], seated` cannot be reproduced. The bracket prints during `Selection` only, because `SIM STATUS` answers in every state and from inside the pods, and a type on the board mid-match would hand a seated pilot the chassis of a contact their own sight still renders `X1`. The observer rule won over the brief's illustration, and the brief's own test item 4 required it. (2) `build_game` is not byte-identical for one input. `build_from`'s dedupe is digest-aware, so an operator pack wearing a shipped `(id, version)` under a different digest is refused rather than silently skipped, on `build_game` as well as `build_fielded`. The brief itself offered this rule ('refused only if the bytes differ'), and C.2's justifying comment cited a `verify_sources` check that never existed. (3) `lobby::leave` runs `reset_stock_tanks` on the emptying release. The brief did not ask for this. Without it, a refused half-refitted `SIM START` followed by everyone leaving handed the next pair a biped, and `refit_chosen_units`' no-rollback rule rested on a false invariant. (4) The fieldable list is in canonical-id order, not the brief's illustrative order. It is the only deterministic order a `BTreeMap` offers, and it is pinned verbatim. (5) The bare `SIM UNIT` also needs a slot, because the sentence reports `Yours:`. A bystander is told to claim first, even in states where claiming is refused; this is left as a note. (6) The brief's statement of known limit 2 is wrong for `line_tank_laser`. That platform carries no cannon, so `choose_weapon`'s first-weapon fallback fires the beam on a bare `FIRE`. The help states the real rule. (7) `@arena/reset`'s tank line gained `, {n} back to their own type`, which the brief did not list. Without it, a pass that rewrote documents would print zero and zero; the line is byte-identical when nothing was refitted. (8) `reset_one`'s old `re-apply the arena pack` refusal is deleted rather than kept beside the refit. A refit handles a version mismatch too, and 16b section 0.2 forbids a second lane. (9) `refit_unit` writes through the governed `persist_unit_document`, not `create_unit`'s raw outbox, and uses `replace_active`, so the lifecycle row and custody marker are kept. Both callers cross a namespace under the `tacsim` identity. (10) The held-billet leg of the `refit_unit` test is proved structurally: a one-op write census plus whole-`SpatialState` equality, with no seat granted. No production path refits a seated unit. (11) One census outside the brief's list moved: `e7c11.rs`'s `UnitStore` writer pin went from seven to eight sites. (12) Deferred and named. The help's `line_tank_laser` line still says 'in place of the cannon's second mount'. A slot cleared to the authored type, or re-claimed, after a refused half-refitted start still fields the refitted tank with no bracket. With `ai_fill` on, a pod whose pilot chose a type and then left or timed out during `Boarding` is filled with the refitted tank. `SimError::Refit` quotes a raw `#N`. The legged cell-cost, `DEFAULT_WEAPON` and AI-fill-type limits are stated, not fixed. `sim_reflex.rs`'s load flake is pre-existing. (13) Doc 16 amendments owed, none of them edited here. Section 3.3: a pod's tank may be refitted for one match, and every return to `Idle`, the last `SIM LEAVE` included, puts the authored type back. Sections 3.7 and 3.8: the seventh `SIM` selector and the refit's place in the `SIM START` order. Section 5: the board bracket and the `SIM UNIT` announce publish both sides' choices during `Selection` only. Section 9: the five items join the fielded catalog, which closes X.1 deviation 1's open question as yes for the fielded game and no for `build_game`. **The closeout pass.** (1) It supersedes Q.8's deviation 5 (the no-slot advice) and deviation 12's first four items (the help's laser line, a cleared or re-claimed slot fielding the refitted tank, the AI fill taking a leaver's refit, `SimError::Refit` quoting a raw object number), and Q.6's deviations 8 and 9 (the unused derives, the two stale five-against-one citations, the three loose carry comments, the unresolved `SIN_ONE` link). (2) Two choices of its own, both said in the doc comments: an UNCLAIMED pod whose tank cannot be put back is logged and skipped where a claimed slot refuses the start, and a non-last `SIM LEAVE` un-fits only the leaver's own pod and never the whole roster. (3) Every doc 16 amendment the judges listed as owed is written in the commit carrying this row. The historical rows of this ledger are NOT rewritten, by decision: the cut A row still prints the old substep quotient, the cut X row still says the mesh is deliberately not jammed and that four cross-tempo tests are owed, and the T, K and S rows still say `tacsim.frame`. They record what was true when they landed; this row and doc 16 carry what is true now. **Still open after cut Q.** (1) Rulings wanted from the owner. (a) The ledger's `blocked` line count is a function of the substep length (`[1, 6, 278, 1]` over the partitions (5000,250), (5000,150), (5000,50) and (370,250)), because `step.rs` journals on the rising edge of the per-substep flag and tells the pilot on the rising edge of `held` (F.2); the remedy, gating the journal on `!was_held` and folding `held` into `state_hash`, changes what the hash covers; the three cadences agree only because they share one partition, and Q.7's blocked-cell memory is idempotent per cell so nothing built depends on the count. (b) The Withdraw stand is gated on full `order_reach_cm`, so a hurt machine with a live line to any classified hostile, the mesh's included, stands and duels and cannot rally while it holds one: bound it by a cornered radius or the engage band, after a playtest. (c) On the web every prefill leaves screen 0 for the scene screen, because that screen mounts the only composer; the TUI keeps the pilot on the cockpit; a screen-local input is a new surface. (d) The absolute substep lattice (plan question 10) is not built. (e) Whether the VPS has Node 22 (plan question 13). (2) Engine carry-forwards. X.6 deviation 13 is half closed: at the stock doctrine the plateau fixture still records seven `Blocked` cells, five on the cliff line, the 1 Hz decide cadence against a 15 m/s tank. A standing `Objective::Withdraw` re-enters `Withdraw` the tick after a rally, flipping to `Patrol` for one tick every `rally_ms` and spending `rallied`. `was` on a `Classified` notice is keyed on the book and not the row, so a once-numbered target re-acquired over the mesh says an `identified` line beside its acquisition. `DesignatorBook` is monotonic and folded row by row into every substep hash (a running digest is the remedy if an hour-long 300-unit match bites). `in_flight` is never cleared after an impact, and `observe_section_lost` is the first hashed mutation made during notice staging, landing in the following substep's hash with no sentence in `hash.rs`. `Arena::set_unit` recomputes the whole band walk on every heat write. `share_pass` pays one unbudgeted extra `in_range` scan per sharer and per mate in a pod-bearing match. `MIN_SUBSTEP_MS` can beat the one-cell bound above 20,000 cm/s on 10 m cells, where motion's guard refuses the jump. `would_overheat` never stages a weapon whose `heat_per_shot` exceeds the platform's whole ceiling. `first_step_is_open` reads only the heading's octant and its doc overstates parity with motion's step 4. The four config validators run with `[tacsim].enabled` off, and their table has no ladder-consistent `quiet_ms` row. `build_game` refuses, where C.2 skipped, an operator pack wearing a shipped id and version under another digest (deliberate). (3) The feed and the ring. The retain rule ignores `PanelFrameKind`, so a future marked `Delta` would evict its own base. `arena.map`'s retain marker is inert today, because a query reply is conn-scoped and never ring-recorded, which makes the clients' 2 s re-ask load-bearing; the mush-session resume paragraph does not mention superseded keyframes. The 256 KiB map bound is a test and not an enforcement, and both query handlers ignore `params` (the `window` parameter and `origin_cell` are reserved for the 10 km path). `PanelStore::forget` is `pub` and drops only `feeds`. `Arena::frame_scan_tables` is `pub` with test callers only. `scan_lines` can emit a 77-column run, and the `CONTACTS` column of four loses its separator at a four-character designator (unreachable for a player). (4) The TUI cockpit. Rows clip and do not wrap, so the scan and damage rows lose their tails in a 34-column pane; an 80-column terminal drops `weapons` and `contacts`, the only selectable pane; the legend still names a marker a later one overwrote; the map gets half the width surplus; the `0 cockpit` tab is sticky until `@notui`; `v` has no grid surface on the TUI; a throttled entry ask is never retried; a world with no tacsim earns one `Unknown query` line; auto-open sends one redundant ask; `docs/interface/02-implementation-plan.md` still says `MAX_SCREENS` is 9 (outside `docs/design`, left for a later cut). (5) The web cockpit. The zoom buttons' glyphs read against convention (their aria-labels are truthful); canvas labels are drawn only at 6 px a cell and up, so not at the default 128-cell view; an unknown scan band letter paints green; `applyMap` lets another match's late answer replace a held map for up to 2 s; the canvas repaints every terrain cell on every keyframe (65,536 rectangles at the 256-cell zoom) and nothing was measured on a full-size arena or a phone; the hello-armed wildcard waits out the latch on a match change inside the first 2 s; the fourteen notice phrasings are the client's own and unread in play; two comments are stale (`TERRAIN_COLOURS` added colours, not classes; the probe whitelists `cockpit` plus `focusComposer`). (6) Deploy. `deploy.sh`'s staleness check is an mtime heuristic over `web/src`: a rollback passes it, it ignores `web/index.html`, `package.json` and the vite config, and it cannot see a gateway binary built before the bundle; its README gives the wrong reason for the ship-the-bundle route, which fails safe; none of it has been executed. Q.3, Q.4 and Q.5 change the gateway half, so dev1 takes them with `systemctl restart dev1mush-gateway` and not a reload; Q.7's binary goes out before the rendered config, because `rally_ms` is a new key under `deny_unknown_fields`, and the template turns `ai_fill` on at the same render. (7) Small stale spots. The `cockpit.rs` module head still says ten verbs and its table lists neither `RANGE` nor `BEARING`; the in-game `cockpit_help` line for `STATUS` omits the callsign; the help never says a designator is case-folded; the help's `SCAN` example wraps at the brief's points, and its sentence that a watched section reads `destroyed` at once holds only for a pilot who already has a reading; `docs/help/simulator.md` states the rally without its arrival and dry conditions; `deliberation::request::BriefContact` carries no condition; Q.8's three known limits (the legged cell cost, the default-weapon rule, AI pods on the authored type) are stated in the help and not fixed. (8) Load-sensitive suites, all green in the sweep and in the closeout gate: `observer_rule` failed once under a builder's or fixer's parallel load at Q.3 (its shooting test) and at Q.4 and Q.7 (`nothing_about_an_undetected_unit_crosses_on_any_surface`); `arena_story_split` once at Q.1; `sim_reflex::with_ai_fill_on_the_default_size_is_the_larger_side` once at Q.8, identically with the change stashed; the cockpit owner-ruling test races a real 3 s provisional window because `classify_after_ms` is content; `sim_weapons`, `arena_story` and `observer_rule` each carry a new timing assumption that fails loudly; `BA` and `X1` are two-character needles in the leak scans, scoped to windows with no echo but more collision-prone than sixteen digits. (9) Test strength. `Hit.` with no held contact is pinned at `notice_line` level only, and `arena_bridge` asserts no survival of `scan_tables`, `condition` or the notice `section` key through emission. `keeping_destroyed` is in no cross-tempo run; `EndReason::Spent` has no cross-tempo test; the second reflex log has no re-engage leg, infers the stand from a `fired` line and lacks the liveness guard. Untested: the `avoid` clause of `first_step_is_open`; the two centre-wide abort sentences, with `NobodyPlaying` unreachable; a stop leaving `carry.turn` alone, a mate exactly at the share reach, and a reversing or westward unit (the Euclidean floor moves its first centimetre one substep early, bounded at 1 cm and partition-independent); the `None` pane fallback of the TUI map; the TUI degrade order behaviourally; two coincident markers, the palette's cover of every terrain glyph and the canvas painting on the web; the full-canonical-id and short-name-collision paths of `resolve_unit_type` and `type_word`; a path from `TacsimConfig` through `verify_sources` to `build_fielded`. The SSH lock witness proves the alias in one branch of its race and its `No such contact.` assertion is unreachable; `sim_solo.rs` makes no cold-platform claim; `two_locks_in_one_pulse_each_name_their_own_contact` no longer name-checks its second line; the TUI observer test does not assert the absence of the pilot name, the team word or a non-`@` glyph before detection, and carries a dead `Mapheading` alternative; the shipped-map round trip decodes with a private copy of `expand_rows`, and the cockpit query's headline equality re-runs the handler's own body; the held-billet leg of the `refit_unit` test is structural, its missing-definition refusal is matched by a loose substring, and two Q.8 assertions are partly tautological. Several final gates' clippy steps were cache hits on a tree already linted. Q.2's 961-byte fall in `ledger_bytes` was attributed to the changed hash being journalled as a variable-width decimal and nobody confirmed it. (10) Two tooling traps for the next briefs: a `\uXXXX` escape written through a JSON tool argument lands as a raw control byte that only `git diff --numstat` reveals, and a `git archive` export built into the repo's own `target/` silently overwrote the release `tacsim`, so give such an export its own `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`. **The owner's rulings** (2026-09-20, on the plan's thirteen questions): "new contact X1 until identified is fine, but if someone locks X1 and it becomes BA then their lock should continue since it is the same contact ... agreed on the rest". Every default of the plan therefore stands. (1) Provisional `X<n>` until classified, then team and slot, `designate_on_detection` off; the lock-continues clause needed no code, because the arena only ever receives a `ContactId`, and Q.6 pins it at arena, cockpit and SSH level. (2) A slot designator plus the public board names the pilot behind a classified contact: accepted, the board is not blanked, and doc 16 §5 says so. (3) A locked contact's range to 10 m, every other's to 50 m. (4) `SCAN` reads a classified contact inside half the effective reach, green at two thirds and yellow at one third, three `[tacsim.sensors]` keys. (5) The section struck is named only for an identified contact. (6) The cockpit on digit 0 in both clients, opened once per match. (7) Surveyed ground: the terrain is every panel client's from the start, the units are not, and the line client's `VIEW` stays 78 by 20. (8) With `ai_fill` on one player alone gets a match, `SIM AI <n>` sizes it, doc 16's minimum side becomes one, and the dev boxes turn the fill on. (9) A hurt machine backs off, shoots back if cornered and returns once after 20 quiet seconds, a released tank does the same, and both sides running dry is a draw. (10) No absolute substep lattice in cut Q. (11) The five extension units join the fielded catalog and `SIM UNIT` offers them to any pilot. (12) Arena notices stay `Sys` lines. (13) The SPA is built on the box, and `deploy.sh` refuses a stale bundle either way. |

doc 16 sections 1, 3.1, 3.3, 3.5 to 3.8, 4, 5, 9 items 1, 4 and 5; owner defaults Q1, Q2, Q3, Q4, Q6, Q12 to Q18, Q28 and Q36 assumed). **The cockpit and the lobby tell the truth: nothing here moves who wins, and all of it is what a pilot reads in the first five minutes.** Five serial sub-cuts. **D.1, the board and the panel**: `SIM STATUS` takes a reader, and while a match runs a player reads their own side in full and every other side as index, callsign and pilot name alone (no pod, no `seated`, no `left`), staff and every other state the whole board; an empty frame weapon list is a fact and not a gap; `STARTUP` reads the mirror first and stamps the one optimistic fact the mirror may hold, a `Starting` hint with four ends: a frame with power up confirms it, a frame carrying the refusal of that `STARTUP` clears it, the clock expires it and an unbind drops it; a contact is never `0 m`; the pilot's band word is `contact_band`, thirds of the lesser of the platform's own nominal sight and the fired weapon's last band, which on every shipped platform is the 600 m sight (close to 200 m, effective to 400 m, far beyond), so the word is scope advice and the help says so, Q18 read plainly, because two words were live before and clamping only the top would have printed the same two; `range_band` stays the commander's word because the order ceiling pinned as its inverse is read by the reflex controller, and the residual is a new 16c row, `T-81`, on E.1's list, with F.3's one-distance sentence suspended by that row; the shared cockpit scene takes the shipped sight and two of its band words move in the JSON and the three goldens, golden text only; the two bare lobby reads answer anybody; the board's clock is the arena's own `now_ms`, carried on `ArenaOut::Frames` and sent when a frame exists or the arena second moved (one a second at Engaged, one a pulse at Alert and Quiet, so the board is at worst a pulse stale and an idle match now sends one small droppable message a pulse), because there is no per-pulse frame to mirror it from; `STATUS` prints the pilot's own scope word, and `T-59` is closed for the printed word only: by Q36 `tempo` stays a frame key and `Frame::same_as` still compares it. **D.2, refusals reach the pilot**: pulse phase 2 stages `Refused` for `Wreck`, `MotionDisabled`, `QueueFull` and `PowerNotOnline` and for nothing else (the epoch purge stays silent), spelling the order with the existing `IntentKind::as_str` in both phases; `QueueFull` is kept against both reviews because the per-pulse bound already drops the order and the notice only stops that being silent, a nine-order beat refuses the ninth at all three cadences under the tree's own zero-time replay driver, and the bound itself is recorded as a debt for cut M; the frame carries `wreck` and `immobile` and the cockpit refuses locally; a destroyed or disabled pilot is told once what the options are; the notice object gains `cause`, `refusal`, `order`, `weapon`, `detail`, `friendly` and `spent` and the web composes its line from them and never from `text`; a round on a spent section is one distinct sentence at most once per 5 s of arena time per section to the victim and to a gunner who holds the target classified, through `spent_hits` (drained in its own substep, unhashed on `lost_designators`' argument) and `spent_said` (persistent, so HASHED, written whether or not anybody is seated, pruned only on touch), while a gunner who holds the target unidentified reads exactly what he reads today, every round, because any rate limit would tell him which rounds struck dead sections; a weapon that makes no heat is never refused at the ceiling, in the engine, the controller and the cockpit alike; every `FIRE` refusal opens `FIRE <weapon> refused.`; `FIRE mg` works by a DERIVED short name (`weapon_initials`, the initials of a multi-word component id) and a unique prefix, a recorded deviation from Q14's content-declared wording because that is six unit pack versions for one word; `(FRIENDLY)` on the lock and hit lines, never a gate; both panels print the scope word. **D.3, the lifecycle's ends**: one `call_off` funnel for every end before `Running`, which `run`'s three early refusals now take and which resets the stock tanks so a refit never outlives its lobby; one writer of `Idle`; `SIM CONCEDE` for a side's lead (`Conceded`, a recorded win for the other side, cells committed) and `[tacsim.match].unattended_ms` (300,000; every slot `left`; ends `Unattended`, outcome `timeout`), both as new `Stop` reasons and so world-driven like `SIM ABORT`, the unattended stamp cleared only by a stop the arena accepted, so a full inbox is retried on the next tick and never disarms the path; a released cold tank starts up and stays in `Withdraw`; a refused check-in releases the seat and retries void; `@arena/release`; six of `T-60`'s eight slips, the other two carried to cut B. **D.4, what a contact is called**: a classified contact that flickers back to a number says `BA is unidentified again: X2.` and the old name is never an alias; `ShadowContact.numbered` makes a mesh re-arrival one line; a platform knows its own receiver is degraded (`UnitState.receiver`, hashed, derived from the jamming fold that the private `Arena::detections` now returns beside its detections, so `jamming_at` still runs once a pass and no perception counter moves; a `receiver` ledger kind on the transition; one `STATUS` line; no source, no bearing, no team and no id, but NOT no count: the integers are the row's own fix and read deeper under stacked emitters, which is declared with the edge-walk as a limit under Q17 and put to the owner with the tightening named); `SectionDestroyed` and `UnitDestroyed` carry `by`, so the gunner's arms stop reading a stale `in_flight`; a lock's range step is derived from the plot's age as the contact's own row already carries it on the same frame, floor 10 m, so it publishes nothing new, and the shared scene's lock line moves from 430 to 440 m in the three goldens. `state_hash` moves in D.2 and D.4 and ledger bytes in D.4, under one rule (persistent state that decides a notice is hashed), and nothing in the tree pins either as a constant. **D.5, the way in**: `l` comes off five exits and pack apply refuses an exit segment equal to the name or alias of a command the kernel or the host registered, the words that work in every room; a module's verb is let through on cut F.4's ruling, because both shipped bus exits are named `board` on purpose and `CockpitGate` stands aside outside the centre for exactly that, and the overlap is counted by a test over the production registry (two rows, both `board`) so the next one is ruled on by a person; Every arena change is a due-event, substep or stop-message fact with its own cross-tempo test (D.2, D.3, D.4), notice lists compared as one ordered list per pilot; every new surface has its observer-rule assertion; D.2 changes `web/` and the TUI (a gateway restart), D.1 and D.4 re-bless golden text and no shipped client file; packs `neighborhood.ooc_area` v5 to v6 and `neighborhood.staff_lobby` v3 to v4 in D.5, taken by a live world by hand with `@atlas/pack plan` and `apply`, OOC first; the harness lens stays 12 ms p99 under `TACSIM_ASSERT=1` with every simulation counter expected to reproduce and only D.4's ledger bytes moving; two rows are owed to 16c with the ledger row, `T-81` and the per-pulse order bound. | | | |

---

## 10. Review (2026-09-16, three independent lenses on Draft 0.1)

Three reviewers read Draft 0.1 of docs 16 and 16b against the tree: an architecture and consistency
lens, a feasibility and sequencing lens, and an adversarial lens. Thirty-five findings; every one is
dispositioned below and, where adopted, folded into Draft 0.2 of both documents. Nothing was rejected
outright; three items are owner questions or deferred.

### Critical

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The in-RAM ledger with per-pulse `moved` events would reach 100+ MB in an hour and the custody-in bundle would exceed the 256 KiB record and 16 MiB bundle ceilings, failing the whole match commit (adversarial, architecture) | **Adopted.** Ledger streamed as 16 KiB chunks through the world task to `mush_core::Ledger`; `moved` replaced by `position_summary` every 5 s; `ledger.max_bytes`, `max_unflushed_chunks`, `match.max_duration_ms`; the result record holds the digest and log id; A2 restated as "memory flat with the ledger streamed". Doc 16 §3.1, §4; cut A5, T4 |
| 2 | The arena transmitting frames through `GatewaySink` cannot resolve pilots in the split server (no `SessionManager` there) and a full channel tears down the whole gateway boundary (adversarial, feasibility, architecture) | **Adopted.** Frames are a seam message: latest-wins slots drained by the world task and emitted through its normal output path as `SendTarget::Char`; the arena has no sink; T's acceptance includes the gateway twin. Doc 16 §3.1, §5; cut T4 |
| 3 | One-jump motion tests only the destination cell, so units tunnel through buildings and water at `Quiet` and `Alert` (adversarial, architecture) | **Adopted.** The pulse is a sequence of substeps bounded to one cell of the fastest unit; per-cell tests; the invariant "no unit occupies an untested cell"; the cross-tempo property test crosses an impassable band. Doc 16 §3.1, §3.4; cut A2, A4, A7 |
| 4 | The world's `+map` frame lists every unit in the arena domain by room visibility, and staff see everything (adversarial) | **Adopted.** A deployed arena's domain is excluded from `visible_rooms`, `units_for`, `+map`, `look` and unit listings for every viewer; A1 asserts the observer rule surface by surface. Doc 16 §1, §3.6; cut S3 |
| 5 | The contact ledger cannot be "reused unchanged": it is a durable action-state family the arena cannot write, its ids are owner-derived not viewer-scoped, and the arena would silently create a second identity space (architecture, adversarial) | **Adopted.** Arena contacts are an in-RAM shadow with their own id type minted from `draw(seed, contact:<viewer>:<target>)`, never crossing to the durable family; one byte-identical refusal; the reuse claim restated as vocabulary and projection rules. Doc 16 §3.6, D-16-10; cut S2 |
| 6 | Cut R's re-point list missed eight test targets and four contract tests that import the rulesets (feasibility) | **Adopted.** R8 lists them; the four contract tests are rewrites. |
| 7 | Cut R's gate step omitted the legacy-handler stanzas and pinned baseline, the restriction-lints rows and census, the module-namespace owners rows and edges, two panic-budget files and a CI line (feasibility) | **Adopted.** R9. |

### High

| # | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|
| 8 | Queued intents survive a seat release because the seam has no revocation and the arena never learns the current epoch; a pilot fires from a pod they left (adversarial, architecture) | **Adopted.** `Control` message; per-`(unit, channel)` epoch table; authorisation at apply; purge on bump; K's verify lens. Doc 16 §3.1, §3.7; cut A3, T3 |
| 9 | Committing post-panic arena state through custody-out inverts the workspace's panic firewall (adversarial) | **Adopted.** A caught panic voids the match; `CustodyIn{Void}` restores check-out snapshots and commits no arena cell. Doc 16 §3.1; §0.6 |
| 10 | Stranded `deployed` markers after a crash or routine hot restart brick every unit that was in a match; no boot sweep specified (adversarial, architecture) | **Adopted.** The marker carries the check-out snapshot; boot reconciliation in the tactics recovery pass restores, clears and releases; a restart-mid-match test. Doc 16 §3.1; cut T5 |
| 11 | The `deployed` refusal was placed at `UnitAuthorityAdapter::prepare_mutation`, which does not exist on `main` (a Codex-branch name); the real admission sites let `@destroy` and `@unit/damage` through mid-match, and one vanished unit would fail the whole check-in (adversarial) | **Adopted.** Real sites named (`TacticsDestroyGate::check`, `TacticsDetachGate::check`, `unit_damage`, `unit_remove`, `@spatial` moves); check-in per-unit fallible with `custody_lost`. Doc 16 §3.1; cut T2 |
| 12 | Check-in wrote back cells but no poses, so world placements stayed at the entry cell for the whole match and after it (adversarial) | **Adopted.** `CustodyIn` carries `final_pose`, applied through `mush_sim::place` in the same action; the placement is documented as frozen while deployed. Doc 16 §3.1; cut T2 and its verify lens |
| 13 | Checking a unit out without keeping it placed drops the pilot's channel grants (`controls_are_live` requires a placed unit), and stations keyed by unit cannot live on the pod (architecture) | **Adopted.** The world placement stays, frozen; stations and grants are on the tank; `unit.status` for a deployed unit specified. Doc 16 §3.1, §3.3, §3.6 |
| 14 | The lifecycle never named the `Boarding → Running` trigger, had no boarding verb, and put check-out in two different places (architecture) | **Adopted.** `BOARD` verb; the trigger (all seated or `match.board_timeout_ms`); check-out on that transition; pre-custody aborts release slots and pods only. Doc 16 §3.7, §3.8; cut K1, K2 |
| 15 | "Wreck" had no M32 representation, and the tanks' provenance and reset were unspecified, so "a second match can start" had no mechanism (architecture) | **Adopted.** Wreck is `hull` condition `wreck` plus a declared `RevokeCapability` set; eight persistent per-pod tanks created once by the arena pack and reset by plan at check-in and `Idle`. Doc 16 §3.3, D-16-6; cut C3, K1 |
| 16 | The plan's harness-on-push CI job contradicts D-H-8 and re-implements `scale_harness`; `ci_gated_test_inventory` would fail unless the target is named in `ci.yml` (feasibility, architecture) | **Adopted as D-16-19.** D-H-8 shape (on demand and scheduled, report-only, `TACSIM_ASSERT=1`); the target named in `ci.yml`; the cost run inside the same target. **Owner question:** if a gating harness in CI is wanted, D-H-8 is superseded and the decision is recorded in D-16-19. Cut H3 |
| 17 | No cut plumbs configuration and deploy files; the live instance and example configs would keep advertising a selector that no longer exists (feasibility) | **Adopted.** Cut G. |
| 18 | The arena is a second intent lane, resolution and RNG beside the shipped `+fire` lane, and the plan's §0.2 forbids exactly that without saying which one dies (feasibility) | **Adopted as D-16-20.** T0 retires `mush-sim::combat`, `tactical_intent` staging, the placeholder resolution, `s9_fire_gate` and the `+fire` stanza; `domaintick` stays for world-side domains with a test that a deployed unit is never in one. |

Doc 16 §4; cut C |

| 20 | Sensor passes batched at pulse end evaluate one instant, so a dash across open ground inside a `Quiet` pulse is never seen; several draws were unkeyed; A7 compared runs within a tempo only (adversarial) | **Adopted.** Passes are due events applied at their timestamps between substeps; the classification gap rule; every draw labelled; A7 is the cross-tempo test. Doc 16 §3.1, §3.5, §3.6; cut A7 |
| 21 | A per-pulse LOS cache keyed by cell pair can almost never hit (one unit per cell, per-unit eye heights) and W4's cache assertion is unmeetable; the reflex controller's path budget is one sixteenth of the arena and `path()` has no production caller (adversarial, architecture) | **Adopted.** No cache; per-observer traces bounded per pulse (`harness.max_traces_per_pulse`); W4's assertion restated; `path_max_cells` raised for the arena; a waypoint graph in the arena pack; the `unreachable` edge; path queries bounded. Doc 16 §2, §3.2, §6; cut N1 |
| 22 | The flight-time formula was off by 100×, and the range the model needs was never defined (the trace's `range_cells` is Chebyshev) (architecture) | **Adopted.** `× 1000`; one Euclidean-centimetre range function; the trace supplies cover and blocking only. Doc 16 §3.2, §3.5 |
| 23 | Only the hit draw had a label; facet, burst and classification draws were undefined, and a long-lived stream violates doc 09 C5 (architecture) | **Adopted.** `draw(seed, label)` with an enumerated, contract-pinned label set; `provider_rng_policy` exemption recorded. Doc 16 §3.5; cut A5, T6 |
| 24 | The candidate cull at `max_range_cells` made any signature factor above 1 unreachable (architecture) | **Adopted.** Cull at `max_range × sensors.max_signature_factor`, declared in config and asserted. Doc 16 §2 W1, §3.6 |

| 30 | A6's phase list had no fire-resolution or impact phase and no per-phase snapshot rule (architecture note) | **Adopted.** Doc 16 §3.1 lists the five phases with their snapshot rules; A6 mirrors it. |

### Confirmed sound by the reviewers, no change

Contextual cockpit verbs on a focused station exist (`CommandReach::Operated`, `resolve_seat`,
`channel_for`) and none of the planned verbs collide with a registered global verb; the `JoinHandle`
and two-shutdown-tail pattern is accurate (`PersistHandles`); `catch_unwind` works because `panic =
"abort"` is deliberately refused by a pinned test; the deletion estimate for the two ruleset crates
(about 63k lines) checks out.

---

## 11. Risks

| Risk | Mitigation |
|---|---|
| Cut R breaks platform tests that used WoD content as a convenience | R8 re-points, never deletes, every test that proved a platform property |
| The world task's output path becomes the arena's bottleneck at 24 pilots and 500 ms | Frames are latest-wins per pilot and dropped when stale; the harness attaches 12 sessions from the first baseline and measures fan-out |
| Custody races (a pilot leaves the pod during check-out, a staff destroy mid-match) | Check-out is one authoritative action; boarding is locked from `Boarding`; the real admission gates refuse deployed units; check-in is per-unit fallible |
| Rate independence silently violated by a later cut | A7's cross-tempo property test is mandatory for every later cut |
| The ledger stream falls behind the world task | The egress ring is bounded; `Lagged` then `Void` are named outcomes; the harness asserts bytes streamed |
| The 2.56 km ceiling is hit by content before the 10 km path lands | The arena pack is authored at 2.56 km; the 10 km addition is designed in doc 16 §11 and re-run through the harness first |
| The centre's rooms in the atlas-owned pack couple the game to `mush-atlas` content ownership | Acceptable under the single-game direction; the tanks and everything mechanical stay in `crates/tacsim/content` |
